-\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pts\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}\,s\hskip 0.85355pts^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,e_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}\hskip 0.28436pts^{\prime}\hskip 0.28436pta^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e^{\prime}}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr).

Obviously, it is also zero. Thus, the whole contribution without the scalar plasmon number densities
{N}^{(1)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}
,
{W}^{(1)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}},\ldots
to equation (
10.6
) turns to zero, and the equation itself, with allowance made for the expressions obtained (
10.9
) – (
10.13
), takes the final form:

\displaystyle\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{\!2}(\tau)}{d\hskip 0.85355pt\tau}=-\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl\{\frac{(2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi)^{3}}{|\Delta{\mathbf{v}}|}\,\sum_{\rho}\,

\displaystyle\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf q}\;\bigl|\hskip 0.85355pt{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k},\,{\bf q}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.85355pt{N}^{(1)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi\hskip 0.85355pt\delta(\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}-{\bf k}\cdot{\bf v}_{\rho}-(-1)^{\rho}\,\Delta{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{q}})\,+

(10.14)

\displaystyle\frac{(2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi)^{3}}{|\Delta{\mathbf{v}}|}\,\sum_{\rho}\hskip 0.56917pt(-1)^{\rho+1}\!\!

\displaystyle\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf q}\;\bigl|\hskip 0.85355pt{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k},\,{\bf q}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.85355pt{W}^{(1)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi\hskip 0.85355pt\delta(\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}-{\bf k}\cdot{\bf v}_{\rho}-(-1)^{\rho}\,\Delta{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{q}})\biggr\}\,\times

\biggl[\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{3}{4}\,({\mathfrak{q}}_{1}+{\mathfrak{q}}_{2})\Lambda^{2}\,+\,\frac{1}{2}\,\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e^{\prime}}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)+\frac{1}{2}\,\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e^{\prime}}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)

+\,3\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}\,e_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e^{\prime}}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,e_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)\biggr].

The equation (
10.14
) like equations (
9.11
), (
10.3
) and (
10.5
) is valid only if the corresponding imaginary parts from the contractions of the type (
9.5
) will be equal to zero. For the previous equations, we have shown that the imaginary parts vanish only for specific values
N_{c}=2
or
N_{c}=3
. Here we will not provide a proof that the imaginary parts for equation (
10.14
), which defines the evolution of
\Lambda^{2}(\tau)
, are also zero for these values due to the cumbersome calculations. Some examples of such calculations are given in the Appendix
F
.

Let us now give the complete system of differential equations for our colorless structures. We introduce the following notation:

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}(\tau)\equiv-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{(2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi)^{3}}{|\Delta{\mathbf{v}}|}\,\sum_{\rho}\,&\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf q}\;\bigl|\hskip 0.85355pt{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k},\,{\bf q}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.85355pt{N}^{(1)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}(\tau)\hskip 1.13791pt2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi\hskip 0.85355pt\delta(\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}-{\bf k}\cdot{\bf v}_{\rho}-(-1)^{\rho}\,\Delta{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{q}})\,+\\[4.30554pt]
\frac{(2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi)^{3}}{|\Delta{\mathbf{v}}|}\,\sum_{\rho}\hskip 0.56917pt(-1)^{\rho+1}\!\!&\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf q}\;\bigl|\hskip 0.85355pt{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k},\,{\bf q}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.85355pt{W}^{(1)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}(\tau)\hskip 1.13791pt2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi\hskip 0.85355pt\delta(\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}-{\bf k}\cdot{\bf v}_{\rho}-(-1)^{\rho}\,\Delta{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{q}})\end{split}

(10.15)

and define the new slow time making use of the formula

\tau^{\prime}=\tau^{\prime}(\tau)=\int\!\mathcal{A}(\tau)d\tau+C_{1},

(10.16)

where
C_{1}
is an arbitrary constant of integration. Considering the change of time variable, the relationship between the functions
{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}
and
{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}
, Eq. (
10.4
), the equations (
9.11
), (
10.3
), (
10.5
) and (
10.14
) acquire a more compact form:

\displaystyle\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}(\tau^{\prime})}{d\hskip 0.85355pt\tau^{\prime}}=\frac{3}{4}\,({\mathfrak{q}}_{1}+C)\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\,+\,\frac{3}{2}\,{\mathfrak{q}}^{2}_{12}\,+\,\frac{1}{2}\,\Lambda^{2},

\displaystyle\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{12}(\tau^{\prime})}{d\hskip 0.85355pt\tau^{\prime}}=\frac{9}{4}\,(2\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}+C)\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{12}-3\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{2},

(10.17)

\displaystyle\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{\!2}(\tau^{\prime})}{d\hskip 0.85355pt\tau^{\prime}}=\frac{3}{4}\,(2\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}+C)\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{2}\,+

\displaystyle\hskip 55.48277pt\frac{1}{2}\,\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e^{\prime}}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)\,+\,\frac{1}{2}\,\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e^{\prime}}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)\,+

\displaystyle\hskip 55.48277pt3\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}\,e_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e^{\prime}}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,e_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr).

We obtained a system of three nonlinear (quadratic) ordinary differential equations of the first order
describing the evolution of certain colorless structures for the
SU(3_{c})
group. As mentioned above,
this system is not a closed one, since two new colorless structures appeared in the last equation for
which it is necessary to define their own equations. But more likely, these equations will contain even
more complex colorless structures. Perhaps this hierarchy of structures would never end and it is necessary
to simply truncate them at some step to obtain a closed system.

11
A system of the first order linear equations

To provide some information regarding the behavior of a solution for the system of kinetic equations (
7.25
), (
7.26
), (
8.7
) and (
8.8
), we consider the model problem of the evolution of the scalar plasmon number densities
N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}},\ N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}},\,W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}
and
W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}
in the case when we neglect a change over time of two averaged color charges
\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{1}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle
and

\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{2}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle
.

For this purpose, we rewrite the system of kinetic equations (
7.25
), (
7.26
), (
8.7
) and (
8.8
) in matrix form. At first we introduce the following notations:

\displaystyle A({\mathbf{k}})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{(2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi)^{3}}{2\hskip 0.85355pt|\Delta{\mathbf{v}}|}\sum_{\rho\hskip 0.85355pt=\hskip 0.85355pt1,\hskip 0.85355pt2}\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf q}\;\bigl|\hskip 0.85355pt{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k},\,{\bf q}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi\hskip 0.56917pt\delta(\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}-{\bf k}\cdot{\bf v}_{\rho}-(-1)^{\rho}\,\Delta{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{q}}),

(11.1)

\displaystyle B({\mathbf{k}})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{(2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi)^{3}}{2\hskip 0.85355pt|\Delta{\mathbf{v}}|}\sum_{\rho\hskip 0.85355pt=\hskip 0.85355pt1,\hskip 0.85355pt2}\!(-1)^{\rho+1}\!\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf q}\;\bigl|\hskip 0.85355pt{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k},\,{\bf q}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi\hskip 0.56917pt\delta(\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}-{\bf k}\cdot{\bf v}_{\rho}-(-1)^{\rho}\,\Delta{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{q}}),

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}(\tau)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{3}{2}\,{\mathfrak{q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{12}\,+\,\frac{3}{4}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}+\frac{9}{4}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{12}\,-\,\Lambda^{2},\quad\;\kappa_{2}(\tau)\equiv\frac{3}{2}\,{\mathfrak{q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{12}\,+\,\frac{3}{4}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}+\frac{9}{4}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{12}\,-\,\Lambda^{2},

then the system (
7.25
), (
7.26
), (
8.7
) and (
8.8
) is rewritten as

\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\!\left(\!\begin{array}[]{c}N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[7.74998pt]
N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[7.74998pt]
W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[7.74998pt]
W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}\end{array}\!\!\right)=\!\left(\!\begin{array}[]{cccc}0&\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}}\,\kappa_{1}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptA({\mathbf{k}})&0&\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}}\,\kappa_{2}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptB({\mathbf{k}})\\[4.30554pt]
\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,\frac{\kappa_{1}(\tau)}{{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}(\tau)}\,A({\mathbf{k}})&0&\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,\frac{\kappa_{1}(\tau)}{{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}(\tau)}\,B({\mathbf{k}})&0\\[4.30554pt]
0&\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}}\,\kappa_{1}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptB({\mathbf{k}})&0&\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}}\,\kappa_{2}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptA({\mathbf{k}})\\[4.30554pt]
\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,\frac{\kappa_{2}(\tau)}{{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(\tau)}\,B({\mathbf{k}})&0&\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,\frac{\kappa_{2}(\tau)}{{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(\tau)}\,A({\mathbf{k}})&0\end{array}\!\right)\!\!\left(\!\begin{array}[]{c}N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[7.74998pt]
N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[7.74998pt]
W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[7.74998pt]
W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}\end{array}\!\!\right)

-\,\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}0&0&0&0\\[4.30554pt]
0&\displaystyle\frac{\partial\ln{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}(\tau)}{\partial\tau}&0&0\\[4.30554pt]
0&0&0&0\\[4.30554pt]
0&0&0&\displaystyle\frac{\partial\ln{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(\tau)}{\partial\tau}\end{array}\right)\!\!\left(\begin{array}[]{c}N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[7.74998pt]
N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[7.74998pt]
W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[7.74998pt]
W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}\end{array}\right).

(11.2)

This evolution equation is an extremely complex, since the combinations
\kappa_{1}(\tau),\,\kappa_{2}(\tau)
,
{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}(\tau)
and
{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(\tau)
themselves obey nonlinear differential equations that follow from the system (
10.17
) and the relation (
10.4
). In order to simplify the problem as much as possible, we further assume that the averaged charges are fixed, i.e. we set

\begin{split}&\kappa_{1}(\tau)=\kappa_{1}(\tau_{0})\equiv\kappa^{0}_{1},\quad\kappa_{2}(\tau)=\kappa_{2}(\tau_{0})\equiv\kappa^{0}_{2},\\[4.30554pt]
&{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}(\tau)={\mathfrak{q}}_{1}(\tau_{0})\equiv{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1},\quad\;{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(\tau)={\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(\tau_{0})\equiv{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{2}.\end{split}

(11.3)

In this case, the system of equations (
11.2
) is noticeably simplified, as it becomes a system of four differential equations with constant coefficients. It is convenient to present this system in block form

\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\!\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[4.30554pt]
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{k}}\end{array}\right)\!=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\mathcal{M}_{11}&\mathcal{M}_{12}\\[4.30554pt]
\mathcal{M}_{21}&\mathcal{M}_{22}\end{array}\right)\!\!\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[4.30554pt]
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{k}}\end{array}\right),

(11.4)

where

{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{k}}\equiv\left(\begin{array}[]{c}N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[4.30554pt]
N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}\end{array}\right),\quad{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{k}}\equiv\left(\begin{array}[]{c}W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}\\[4.30554pt]
W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}\end{array}\right)

and

\begin{array}[]{llll}&\mathcal{M}_{11}\equiv\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}}\,\kappa^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptA({\mathbf{k}})\\[4.30554pt]
\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,\frac{\kappa^{0}_{1}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\,A({\mathbf{k}})&0\end{array}\right),&\mathcal{M}_{12}\equiv\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}}\,\kappa^{0}_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptB({\mathbf{k}})\\[4.30554pt]
\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,\frac{\kappa^{0}_{1}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\,B({\mathbf{k}})&0\end{array}\right),\\[25.83325pt]
&\mathcal{M}_{21}\equiv\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}}\,\kappa^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptB({\mathbf{k}})\\[4.30554pt]
\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,\frac{\kappa^{0}_{2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{2}}\,B({\mathbf{k}})&0\end{array}\right),&\mathcal{M}_{22}\equiv\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}}\,\kappa^{0}_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptA({\mathbf{k}})\\[4.30554pt]
\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,\frac{\kappa^{0}_{2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{2}}\,A({\mathbf{k}})&0\end{array}\right).\end{array}

(11.5)

To construct the general solution of the homogeneous system (
11.4
), it is necessary first to determine the eigenvalues
\lambda_{i},\,i=1,\ldots 4,
by solving the characteristic equation

\det\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda&\mathcal{M}_{12}\\[4.30554pt]
\mathcal{M}_{21}&\mathcal{M}_{22}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda\end{array}\right)=0,

(11.6)

where
E
is the identity
2\times 2
matrix. For the sake of definiteness, we consider that the determinant of the matrix
\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.56917pt\lambda
is nonzero, that is

\det(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)=\lambda^{2}\,-\,\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}N_{c}}\,\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\,A^{2}({\mathbf{k}})\equiv

(11.7)

\biggl[\lambda\,+\,\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d_{A}N_{c})^{1/2}}\,\biggl(\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!A({\mathbf{k}})\biggr]\biggl[\lambda\,-\,\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d_{A}N_{c})^{1/2}}\,\biggl(\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!A({\mathbf{k}})\biggr]\,\neq\,0.

This allow us to evaluate the expression (
11.6
) using Schur’s formula for the determinant of a block matrix
[
60
]
. In our case, we have

\det\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda&\mathcal{M}_{12}\\[4.30554pt]
\mathcal{M}_{21}&\mathcal{M}_{22}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda\end{array}\right)=

(11.8)

\det(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)\,\det\Bigl[(\mathcal{M}_{22}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)-\mathcal{M}_{21}\hskip 0.28436pt(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{12}\Bigr]=0.

Here, the inverse matrix
(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.85355pt\lambda)^{-1}
has the form

(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)^{-1}=\frac{1}{\det(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.85355pt\lambda)}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\lambda&-\hskip 0.85355pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}}\,\kappa^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptA({\mathbf{k}})\\[4.30554pt]
-\hskip 0.85355pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,\frac{\kappa^{0}_{1}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\,A({\mathbf{k}})&-\lambda\end{array}\right).

(11.9)

By using the explicit form of the matrices
\mathcal{M}_{21}
and
\mathcal{M}_{12}
, Eq. (
11.5
), and of the inverse matrix (
11.9
), we are able to determine the product of three matrices on the right-hand side of (
11.8
)

\mathcal{M}_{21}(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.85355pt\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{12}=

\frac{1}{\det(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)}\,\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}N_{c}}\,B^{2}({\mathbf{k}})\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\lambda\,\displaystyle\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}&-\hskip 0.56917pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}}\,\displaystyle\frac{\kappa^{0}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}A({\mathbf{k}})\\[8.61108pt]
-\hskip 0.56917pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,\displaystyle\frac{\kappa^{0}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{2}}A({\mathbf{k}})&-\lambda\,\displaystyle\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{2})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{2}}\end{array}\right).

Let us substitute the last expression into equation (
11.8
). After simple algebraic transformations, the characteristic equation (
11.8
) can be reduced to the following expression:

\det(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.85355pt\lambda)\det\Bigl[(\mathcal{M}_{22}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)-\mathcal{M}_{21}\hskip 0.28436pt(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{12}\Bigr]=

(11.10)

\frac{1}{\det(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.85355pt\lambda)}\,\biggl[\lambda^{2}\biggl(\det(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)-\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}N_{c}}\,\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\,B^{2}({\mathbf{k}})\biggr)\biggl(\det(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)-\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}N_{c}}\,\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{2})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{2}}\,B^{2}({\mathbf{k}})\biggr)-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}N_{c}}\,\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{2})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{2}}\,A^{2}({\mathbf{k}})\biggl(\det(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)+\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}N_{c}}\,\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\,B^{2}({\mathbf{k}})\biggr)^{\!\!2}\,\biggr]=0.

Since, in general, determining the roots here is quite difficult, we simplify the task by an additional special assumption that

\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{2})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{2}}\,=\,\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}.

Then the relation (
11.10
) can be factored into a product of two polynomials:

\displaystyle\biggl(\det(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)\,

\displaystyle\biggl[\lambda\,-\,\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d_{A}N_{c})^{1/2}}\,\biggl(\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!A({\mathbf{k}})\biggr]\,-

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}N_{c}}\,\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\,B^{2}({\mathbf{k}})

\displaystyle\biggl[\lambda\,+\,\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d_{A}N_{c})^{1/2}}\,\biggl(\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!A({\mathbf{k}})\biggr]\biggr)\times

\displaystyle\biggl(\det(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)\,

\displaystyle\biggl[\lambda\,+\,\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d_{A}N_{c})^{1/2}}\,\biggl(\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!A({\mathbf{k}})\biggr]\,-

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{A}N_{c}}\,\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\,B^{2}({\mathbf{k}})

\displaystyle\biggl[\lambda\,-\,\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d_{A}N_{c})^{1/2}}\,\biggl(\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!A({\mathbf{k}})\biggr]\biggr)=0.

Based on the representation (
11.7
) for the determinant
\det(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)
, from the preceding expression it is not difficult to find the desired eigenvalues
\lambda_{i}
:

\begin{split}&\lambda_{1}=\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d_{A}N_{c})^{1/2}}\,\biggl(\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\bigl[A({\mathbf{k}})+B({\mathbf{k}})\bigr],\\[4.30554pt]
&\lambda_{2}=\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d_{A}N_{c})^{1/2}}\,\biggl(\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\bigl[A({\mathbf{k}})-B({\mathbf{k}})\bigr],\\[4.30554pt]
&\lambda_{3}=-\hskip 0.56917pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d_{A}N_{c})^{1/2}}\,\biggl(\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\bigl[A({\mathbf{k}})-B({\mathbf{k}})\bigr],\\[4.30554pt]
&\lambda_{4}=-\hskip 0.56917pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d_{A}N_{c})^{1/2}}\,\biggl(\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\bigl[A({\mathbf{k}})+B({\mathbf{k}})\bigr].\end{split}

(11.11)

Obviously, the following relations hold

\lambda_{3}=-\lambda_{2},\quad\lambda_{4}=-\lambda_{1}.

(11.12)

Further, by virtue of the definition of the functions
A({\mathbf{k}})
and
B({\mathbf{k}})
, Eq. (
11.1
), we have

\displaystyle A({\mathbf{k}})+B({\mathbf{k}})=\frac{(2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi)^{3}}{|\Delta{\mathbf{v}}|}\,\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf q}\;\bigl|\hskip 0.85355pt{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1)}_{\;{\bf k},\,{\bf q}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi\hskip 0.56917pt\delta(\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}-{\bf k}\cdot{\bf v}_{1}+\Delta{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{q}}),

\displaystyle A({\mathbf{k}})-B({\mathbf{k}})=\frac{(2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi)^{3}}{|\Delta{\mathbf{v}}|}\,\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf q}\;\bigl|\hskip 0.85355pt{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\;{\bf k},\,{\bf q}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,2\hskip 0.56917pt\pi\hskip 0.56917pt\delta(\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}-{\bf k}\cdot{\bf v}_{2}-\Delta{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{q}}),

and therefore

\lambda_{1}>0,\quad\lambda_{2}>0.

Let us construct the eigenvectors corresponding to the eigenvalues (
11.11
). For this purpose, we solve the homogeneous algebraic system

\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda&\mathcal{M}_{12}\\[4.30554pt]
\mathcal{M}_{21}&\mathcal{M}_{22}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathbf{X}}\\[4.30554pt]
{\mathbf{Y}}\end{array}\right)=0,

(11.13)

where
{\mathbf{X}}
and
{\mathbf{Y}}
are two-component vectors. Let us examine this system in more detail

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathbf{X}}+\mathcal{M}_{12}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathbf{Y}}=0,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{21}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathbf{X}}+(\mathcal{M}_{22}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathbf{Y}}=0.

From the first equation we define

{\mathbf{X}}=-\hskip 0.85355pt(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{12}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathbf{Y}}

(11.14)

and substitute it into the second equation and as a result,

\Bigl((\mathcal{M}_{22}\,-\,E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)-\mathcal{M}_{21}\,(\mathcal{M}_{11}-E\hskip 0.28436pt\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{12}\Bigr)\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathbf{Y}}=0.

Taking into account the specific expressions for the eigenvalues
\lambda=\lambda_{i},\,i=1,\ldots,4
, as were defined by Eq. (
11.11
), it is not difficult to obtain the explicit form for
{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{Y}}^{(\lambda_{i})}
:

{\mathbf{Y}}^{(\lambda_{1})}={\mathbf{Y}}^{(\lambda_{2})}=\left(\!\begin{array}[]{c}1\\[4.30554pt]
\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1})^{1/2}}\end{array}\!\right)Y_{1},\qquad{\mathbf{Y}}^{(\lambda_{3})}={\mathbf{Y}}^{(\lambda_{4})}=\left(\!\begin{array}[]{c}1\\[4.30554pt]
-\hskip 0.56917pt\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1})^{1/2}}\end{array}\!\right)Y_{1},

where
Y_{1}
is an arbitrary parameter. Further substituting the obtained expressions
{\mathbf{Y}}^{(\lambda_{i})}
into the right-hand side of equation
(
11.14
), we find the remaining two-component vectors
{\mathbf{X}}^{(\lambda_{i})}
:

{\mathbf{X}}^{(\lambda_{1})}={\mathbf{Y}}^{(\lambda_{1})},\quad{\mathbf{X}}^{(\lambda_{2})}=-{\mathbf{Y}}^{(\lambda_{2})},\quad{\mathbf{X}}^{(\lambda_{3})}=-{\mathbf{Y}}^{(\lambda_{3})},\quad{\mathbf{X}}^{(\lambda_{4})}={\mathbf{Y}}^{(\lambda_{4})}.

The two-component vectors
{\mathbf{X}}^{(\lambda_{i})}
and
{\mathbf{Y}}^{(\lambda_{i})}
enable us to obtain a set of four linear independent eigenvectors
{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}^{(\lambda_{i})}=({\mathbf{X}}^{(\lambda_{i})}\;{\mathbf{Y}}^{(\lambda_{i})})^{T}
, describing the structure of solutions to the matrix equation (
11.13
). The superscript
T
denotes the transpose. Setting
Y_{1}=1
, here we have

\begin{split}&{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}^{(\lambda_{1})}=\left(\!\begin{array}[]{c}1\\[4.30554pt]
\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1})^{1/2}}\\[8.61108pt]
1\\[4.30554pt]
\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1})^{1/2}}\end{array}\!\right),\qquad\;\;\,{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}^{(\lambda_{2})}=\left(\!\begin{array}[]{c}-\hskip 0.85355pt1\\[4.30554pt]
-\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1})^{1/2}}\\[8.61108pt]
1\\[4.30554pt]
\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1})^{1/2}}\end{array}\!\right),\\[4.30554pt]
&{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}^{(\lambda_{3})}=\left(\!\begin{array}[]{c}-\hskip 0.85355pt1\\[4.30554pt]
\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1})^{1/2}}\\[8.61108pt]
1\\[4.30554pt]
-\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1})^{1/2}}\end{array}\!\right),\qquad{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}^{(\lambda_{4})}=\left(\!\begin{array}[]{c}1\\[4.30554pt]
-\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1})^{1/2}}\\[8.61108pt]
1\\[4.30554pt]
-\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1})^{1/2}}\end{array}\!\right).\end{split}

(11.15)

The general solution of a system of linear homogeneous ordinary differential equations (
11.4
) can be expressed as a linear decomposition of basis solutions derived from the eigenvalues (
11.11
) and eigenvectors: (
11.15
)

\left(\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{k}}(\tau)\\[4.30554pt]
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{k}}(\tau)\end{array}\!\!\right)\!\,=\,C_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{\lambda_{1}\tau}\,{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}^{(\lambda_{1})}\,+\,C_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{\lambda_{2}\tau}\,{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}^{(\lambda_{2})}\,+\,C_{3}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\lambda_{2}\tau}\,{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}^{(\lambda_{3})}\,+\,C_{4}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\lambda_{1}\tau}\,{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}^{(\lambda_{4})}.

(11.16)

Here,
C_{i},\,i=1,\ldots,4
is some arbitrary constants and we have used the relation (
11.12
) between the eigenvalues
\lambda_{i}
. The constants
C_{i}
can be expressed in terms of the initial values of the plasmon number densities

{\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)=\left(\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\\[4.30554pt]
N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\end{array}\!\!\right),\quad{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)=\left(\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\\[4.30554pt]
W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\end{array}\!\!\right).

According to the representation (
11.15
), we need to solve the following algebraic system with respect to the constants
C_{i}

\displaystyle C_{1}\,-\,C_{2}\,-\,C_{3}\,+\,C_{4}\,=N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0),

\displaystyle C_{1}\,-\,C_{2}\,+\,C_{3}\,-\,C_{4}\,=N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2},

\displaystyle C_{1}\,+\,C_{2}\,+\,C_{3}\,+\,C_{4}\,=W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0),

\displaystyle C_{1}\,+\,C_{2}\,-\,C_{3}\,-\,C_{4}\,=W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}.

The solution has the following form:

\displaystyle C_{1}=\frac{1}{4}\,\biggl[N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)+N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}+W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)+W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}\biggr],

\displaystyle C_{2}=-\frac{1}{4}\,\biggl[N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)+N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}-W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)-W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}\biggr],

\displaystyle C_{3}=-\frac{1}{4}\,\biggl[N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)-N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}-W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)+W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}\biggr],

\displaystyle C_{4}=\frac{1}{4}\,\biggl[N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)-N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}+W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)-W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}\biggr].

Substituting these solutions into the general one (
11.16
), using the explicit form of eigenvectors (
11.15
) and collecting similar terms, we find the required solutions of the original system (
11.4
) in a rather compact form. The first pair of solutions has the form

\begin{split}&N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(\tau)\,=\,\\
&\cosh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\cosh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptN^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\,+\,\sinh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\cosh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptN^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}\,+\\
&\sinh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\sinh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptW^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\,+\,\cosh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\sinh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptW^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2},\\[21.52771pt]
&N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(\tau)\,=\,\\
&\sinh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\cosh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptN^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}}\,+\,\cosh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\cosh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptN^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\,+\\
&\cosh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\sinh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptW^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}}\,+\,\sinh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\sinh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptW^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\end{split}

(11.17)

and the second one is

\begin{split}&W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(\tau)\,=\,\\
&\sinh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\sinh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptN^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\,+\,\cosh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\sinh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptN^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}\,+\\[4.30554pt]
&\cosh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\cosh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptW^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\,+\,\sinh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\cosh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptW^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2},\\[21.52771pt]
&W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(\tau)\,=\,\\
&\cosh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\sinh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptN^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}}\,+\,\sinh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\sinh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptN^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\,+\\[4.30554pt]
&\sinh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\cosh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptW^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)\biggl(\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!\!\frac{1}{{\bigl(\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{1/2}}\,+\,\cosh(\lambda_{+}\tau)\cosh(\lambda_{-}\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptW^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0),\end{split}

(11.18)

where we have used the hyperbolic sine and cosine sum/difference the formulas and introduced the notations

\lambda_{+}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\,(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d_{A}N_{c})^{1/2}}\,\biggl(\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!A({\mathbf{k}}),

\lambda_{-}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\,(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})=\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d_{A}N_{c})^{1/2}}\,\biggl(\frac{(\kappa^{0}_{1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{1}}\biggr)^{\!1/2}\!B({\mathbf{k}}).

Here, on the right-hand side we considered the definitions of eigenvalues
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
, Eq. (
11.11
). Taking into account the explicit form of the functions
A({\mathbf{k}})
and
B({\mathbf{k}})
, Eq. (
11.1
), we see that the sum of eigenvalues
\lambda_{+}=\lambda_{+}({\mathbf{k}})
is positive for all values of the wave vector
{\mathbf{k}}
, while the difference
\lambda_{-}=\lambda_{-}({\mathbf{k}})
is, generally speaking, indefinite function.

As the initial values for the scalar plasmon number densities, it is most natural to choose

N^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)=W^{(2)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)=0,

that is, we assume the ‘‘color’’ part to be zero at the initial moment of time, and as
N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)
and
W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)
we choose thermal equilibrium value in the form of the Planck distribution function

N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)=W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}(0)=\frac{1}{{\rm e}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\omega^{l}_{\mathbf{k}}/k_{B}T^{\ast}}-1},

where
T^{\ast}
is a certain constant, which can be interpreted as a plasmon gas temperature in the statistical equilibrium state. From the solutions obtained (
11.17
) and (
11.18
), we see that even if there are no ‘‘color’’ plasma excitations at the initial moment, they are inevitably generated during bremsstrahlung in scattering of hard color-charged particles on each other.

The exact solutions (
11.17
) and (
11.18
), rather simple in structure, are obtained in the approximation when certain combinations of the averaged color charges in the original system of equations are fixed (
11.3
). Consideration of the evolution of these combinations can drastically changes the solutions found. For this, it is necessary to solve a system of nonlinear (quadratic) ordinary differential equations (
10.17
)
with constant coefficients and then return to the originally time
\tau
through the relations (
10.15
) and (
10.16
). The scalar plasmon number densities
N^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}
and
W^{(1)}_{\mathbf{k}}
enter into the solution in an integrated manner. In order to obtain an analytical solution, here it will also be necessary to make simplifying assumptions.

12
Conclusion

In this study, within the framework of the classical Hamiltonian formalism
the general theory of calculation of the effective amplitude
{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf k},\,{\bf q}}(t)
describing the plasmon bremsstrahlung in collision of a high-energy color-charged particle with thermal partons in a hot quark-gluon plasma, is developed. Our approach is actually based on three main assumptions. The first of them is associated with the representation of original normal field variable
a^{\hskip 0.56917pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}
in a ‘‘split’’ form, i.e. as a sum of two independent components
a^{\hskip 0.56917pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}
and

a^{\hskip 0.56917pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}
. Intuitively, it can be understood as the sum of two contributions to the radiation field from the emission of bremsstrahlung radiation of two independent hard particles. Other main more nontrivial assumption concerns the structure of the free Hamiltonian
H^{(0)}
, Eq. (
2.9
). This Hamiltonian is not defined in terms of the original field variable
a^{\hskip 0.56917pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}
, as is usually done (see, for example
[
42
,
1
]
), but in terms of its independent components
a^{\hskip 0.56917pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}
and

a^{\hskip 0.56917pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}
. Finally, the third and last assumption is related to two decompositions of the general expression for the matrix plasmon number density
{\mathcal{N}}^{\,(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\!(\tau)
, defined by the definition (
6.2
). The decompositions were defined in effective two-particle and color spaces, and represented by the expressions (
6.7
) and (
7.1
), respectively. The first two of assumptions are fairly straightforward and natural, and the third is not. They turned out to be quite sufficient to build the required classical Hamiltonian formalism for the radiation process in question.

In the present work we restricted ourselves to the detailed discussion of only the simplest process of nonlinear interaction of soft purely collective boson excitations in the quark-gluon plasma: the tree-level order plasmon bremsstrahlung by a hard color-charged particle, i.e.

{\rm G}_{1}+{\rm G}_{2}\rightarrow{\rm G}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}+{\rm G}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{2}+{\rm g}^{\ast},

(12.1)

where
{\rm g}^{\ast}
is a plasmon collective excitation and
{\rm G}_{1,2},\,{\rm G}^{\prime}_{1,2}
are excitations with the characteristic momenta of order of the temperature
T
and above. For the weakly-excited system corresponding to the level of thermal fluctuations, the given radiation process is dominant. The approach developed allows us to consider more complicated radiation processes, for example, bremsstrahlung of two plasmons

{\rm G}_{1}+{\rm G}_{2}\rightarrow{\rm G}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{1}+{\rm G}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{2}+{\rm g}^{\ast}_{1}+{\rm g}^{\ast}_{2}.

The corresponding effective Hamiltonian of this radiation process has the following form

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}^{(6)}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\rho_{1},\hskip 0.85355pt\rho_{2}}\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho_{1}\rho_{2})\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}\hskip 0.85355pta_{4}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,c^{\hskip 0.56917pt(\rho_{1})\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\hskip 0.56917pt(\rho_{2})\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{4}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\,+

\displaystyle\sum_{\rho_{1},\hskip 0.85355pt\rho_{2}}\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho_{1}\rho_{2})\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}\hskip 0.85355pta_{4}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!(\rho_{1})\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\ast\ \!\!(\rho_{2})\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{4}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}.

The practical computation of the effective amplitude
{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho_{1}\rho_{2})\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}\hskip 0.85355pta_{4}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and the corresponding kinetic equations becomes considerably more cumbersome and, as a consequence, ineffective. Other approaches that are not directly related to the Hamiltonian formalism are more appropriate here (see for example
[
61
]
).

However, even for the simplest bremsstrahlung radiation process (
12.1
) the story does not end here. In fact, the most general form of the effective Hamiltonian describing one-plasmon bremsstrahlung in scattering of an high-energy color-charged particle by a hard thermal color-charged one, instead of (
4.5
), is

{\mathcal{H}}=\sum_{\rho}\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\;{\bf k}}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2})\,c^{\hskip 0.56917pt(\rho)\hskip 0.56917pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\,+\,\sum_{\rho}\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\;{\bf k}}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2})\,c^{\ast\ \!\!(\rho)\hskip 0.56917pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}},

where the effective amplitude
{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\;{\bf k}}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2})
is an arbitrary function of the color vectors
\mathcal{Q}_{1}=(\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1})
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}=(\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2})
. We can represent this amplitude by a series expansion in the color charges
\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}
of two energetic particles

{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\;{\bf k}}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2})={T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}}\,{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\,+

(12.2)

\frac{1}{2}\,\Bigl({T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}}\,{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}+{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}}\,{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\Bigr)+\hskip 0.85355pt\ldots\,.

Thus, our expression for the effective amplitude (
4.6
) – (
4.9
) represents only the first term of this expansion. It is implicitly assumed that the remaining higher-order terms of the expansion are suppressed by the coupling constant. However, what is their physical meaning or interpretation? At least the next contribution in the expansion (
12.2
) can be calculated explicitly within the framework of our approach. An example of calculating the next-to-leading effective amplitude can be found in
[
61
]
. The diagrammatic interpretation of certain terms in
{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}}
is presented in Fig.
4
.

\!{\mathbf{+}}

\!{\mathbf{+}}

Figure 4:
Some soft one-loop corrections to the tree-level plasmon bremsstrahlung process depicted in Fig.
2
.

From the view of these diagrams, we can conclude that what is being discussed here is soft ‘‘one-loop’’ corrections to the tree-level plasmon bremsstrahlung radiation. These one-loop corrections are suppressed by a power of
g^{2}
compared with the tree approximation (
4.6
) – (
4.9
). The next-to-leading order effective amplitude
{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}}
in (
12.2
) must have the following structure, instead of (
4.6
):

{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}}=\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf q}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf q}_{2}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\phantom{\prime}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\phantom{\prime}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{\;{\bf k},\,{\bf q}_{1},\,{\bf q}_{2}},

i.e., here we have two momentum transfer
{\bf q}_{1}
and
{\bf q}_{2}
. We assume that the higher-order terms in the expansion (
12.2
) are a kind of fluctuation deviation from the bremsstrahlung radiation in the tree-level approximation. In particular, the first diagram in Fig.
4
represents the one-loop correction to the propagator of hard particle with the label 1, defined by the expression (
4.10
).

A generalization of the results obtained in the present study to the fermionic sector of hard and soft excitations in the quark-gluon plasma is of significant theoretical and practical interest. It is clear that to describe the color degrees of freedom of both the hard color-charged particles with half-integer spin and the soft normal fermionic field variables within the framework of classical Hamiltonian formalism, we need to use functions taking values in Grassmann algebra. In constructing a complete Hamiltonian wave theory of QGP including bosonic and fermionic, as well as hard and soft degrees of freedom it will be necessary to define a generalized (nonlinear) system of dynamical equations of the Wong type describing the evolution of both the ordinary (commutative) classical color charge
\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.56917pta}
of a hard test particle with integer spin and the Grassmann-valued color charges
{\uptheta}^{\,\ast\ \!\!i}
and
{\uptheta}^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!i},\,i=1,\ldots,N_{c}
, of other hard test particle with half-integer spin in external stochastic bosonic and fermionic fields. The Grassmann color charges belong to the
fundamental
representation of the
SU(N_{c})
color group. Here, it will also be necessary to generalize the corresponding canonical transformations, which include simultaneously the boson and fermion degrees of freedom of the collective excitations of the quark-gluon plasma, and the degrees of freedom associated with the commutative and anticommutative color charges of hard particles.

It should be noted, however, that the consideration of radiative processes that change the statistics of soft and hard modes seems to be quite complicated and involved. Here, we need to create an appropriate apparatus that adequately addresses this problem (see, for example,
[
62
,
63
]
). It can be assumed that the structure of the effective Hamiltonian describing bremsstrahlung process of purely collective soft fermion excitations (plasminos) will be noticeably more complicated than the effective Hamiltonian for soft boson excitations obtained in the present work, Eq. (
4.5
). Such an effective Hamiltonian should most likely have the following form:

\begin{split}{\mathcal{H}}^{(5)}=&\sum_{\rho}\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pti\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf p}}\,b^{\hskip 0.56917pt(\rho)\hskip 0.56917pti}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\uptheta}^{\,\ast\ \!\!i_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\,+\,\sum_{\rho}\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pti\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf p}}\,b^{\ast\ \!\!(\rho)\hskip 0.56917pti}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\uptheta}^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!i_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\,+\\[4.30554pt]
&\sum_{\rho}\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pti\,i_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{\;{\bf p}}\,b^{\hskip 0.56917pt(\rho)\hskip 0.56917pti}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\uptheta}^{\,\ast\ \!\!i_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\,+\,\sum_{\rho}\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pti\,i_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{\;{\bf p}}\,b^{\ast\ \!\!(\rho)\hskip 0.56917pti}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\uptheta}^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!i_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}.\end{split}

(12.3)

Here, the Grassmann-valued functions
b^{\hskip 0.56917pt(\rho)\hskip 0.56917pti}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}},\,\rho=1,2,
are the independent components of the total normal fermion field variable
b^{\hskip 0.56917pti}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}}
, i.e.

b^{\hskip 0.56917pti}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}}=b^{\hskip 0.56917pt(1)\hskip 0.56917pti}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}}+b^{\hskip 0.56917pt(2)\hskip 0.56917pti}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}},

which in turn is amplitude for the plasmino mode excitations as it follows from the decomposition of the collective quark-antiquark field into plane waves

{\Psi}^{i}_{\alpha}(x)\!=\!\sum_{\;\lambda\hskip 0.56917pt=\hskip 0.56917pt\pm\hskip 0.56917pt1}\hskip 0.28436pt\int\!\!\frac{d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf p}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\left(\!\frac{Z_{-}({\bf p})}{2}\right)^{\!\!1/2}\!\left[\hskip 0.85355ptb_{\bf p}^{\,i}(\lambda)\hskip 0.85355ptu^{(-)}_{\alpha}(\hat{\bf p},\lambda)\,e^{-i\hskip 0.85355pt\omega^{-}_{{\bf p}}\hskip 0.56917ptt\hskip 0.85355pt+\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}\hskip 0.56917pt\cdot\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf x}}\!+d_{\bf p}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pti}(\lambda)\hskip 0.85355ptv^{(-)}_{\alpha}(\hat{\bf p},\lambda)\,e^{\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 0.85355pt\omega^{-}_{{\bf p}}\hskip 0.56917ptt\hskip 0.85355pt-\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf p}\hskip 0.56917pt\cdot\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf x}}\hskip 0.85355pt\right]\!,

where
\alpha=1,2,3,4
, the spinors
u^{(s)}_{\alpha}(\hat{\bf p},\lambda)
and
v^{(s)}_{\alpha}(\hat{\bf p},\lambda)
denote solutions of the free massless Dirac equation and
\omega^{-}_{{\bf p}}
is the dispersion relation of plasmino mode. The Hamiltonian (
12.3
) describes radiative process with the change of statistics that occurs when two color-charged particles with integer and half-integer spins collide with each other.

One more remark should be made regarding one-plasmon bremsstrahlung.
To the effective Hamiltonian (
4.5
), we need to add another Hamiltonian that is associated with the hard Fermi excitations of the quark-gluon plasma, namely

{\mathcal{H}}^{(5)}=\sum_{\rho}\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}}\,c^{\hskip 0.56917pt(\rho)\hskip 0.56917pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.56917pt\hskip 0.56917pt{\uptheta}^{\,\ast\ \!\!i_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt{\uptheta}^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!i_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\,+\,\sum_{\rho}\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)\hskip 0.85355pta\,i_{1}\hskip 0.85355pti_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!(\rho)\hskip 0.56917pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\uptheta}^{\,\ast\ \!\!i_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\hskip 0.56917pt{\uptheta}^{\phantom{\ast}\!\!i_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}.

It will allow to take into account all relevant contributions to the single plasmon bremsstrahlung process.

As an application of the effective Hamilton theory of plasmon (and plasmino) bremsstrahlung produced by a hard color-charged particle propagating in the hot quark-gluon plasma, we intend to consider the application to the problem of energy losses of the fast particle. This task will be the subject of our next work and be carried out within the framework of the basic ideas of the paper
[
64
]
, i.e. with the help of the construction of corresponding classical scattering matrix
[
65
,
66
,
67
]
. However, the direct transference of ideas in
[
64
]
meets with certain difficulties because the approach developed in the present study requires some ‘‘adjustment and adaptation’’ to the multiple-time-scale perturbation method, which we have used extensively throughout the paper
[
64
]
.

Appendix A
Effective three-gluon vertex

In this Appendix, we have provided the explicit form of the vertex functions and gluon propagator in the hard thermal loop (HTL) approximation
[
68
,
69
,
70
]
.

Effective three-gluon vertex

\,{}^{\ast}\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\rho}(k,k_{1},k_{2})\equiv\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\rho}(k,k_{1},k_{2})+\delta\hskip 0.71114pt\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\rho}(k,k_{1},k_{2})

(A.1)

is the sum of bare three-gluon vertex

\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\hskip 0.56917pt\rho}(k,k_{1},k_{2})=g^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}(k-k_{1})^{\rho}+g^{\hskip 0.85355pt\nu\hskip 0.56917pt\rho}(k_{1}-k_{2})^{\mu}+g^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\rho}(k_{2}-k)^{\nu}

(A.2)

and the corresponding HTL correction

\delta\hskip 0.71114pt\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\rho}(k,k_{1},k_{2})=3\hskip 0.99594pt\omega^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}_{\rm pl}\!\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.99594pt\Omega}{4\pi}\,\frac{v^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu}\hskip 0.56917ptv^{\hskip 0.85355pt\nu}v^{\hskip 0.85355pt\rho}}{v\cdot k+i\hskip 0.71114pt\epsilon}\,\Biggl(\frac{\omega_{2}}{v\cdot k_{2}-i\epsilon}-\frac{\omega_{1}}{v\cdot k_{1}-i\epsilon}\Biggr),\quad\epsilon\rightarrow+\hskip 0.56917pt0,

(A.3)

where
v^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu}=(1,{\bf{\bf v}})
,
k^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu}=(\omega,{\bf k})
is a gluon four-momentum with
k+k_{1}+k_{2}=0
,
d\hskip 0.99594pt\Omega
is a differential solid angle and
\omega_{\rm pl}^{2}=g^{2}(2N_{c}+N_{f})T^{2}/18
is the quark-gluon plasma frequency squared. We present below useful properties of the three-gluon HTL-resummed vertex function for complex conjugation and permutation of momenta:

\left(\!\,{}^{\ast}\Gamma_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}(-k_{1}-k_{2},k_{1},k_{2})\right)^{\ast}=-\!\,^{\ast}\Gamma_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}(k_{1}+k_{2},-k_{1},-k_{2})=\!\,^{\ast}\Gamma_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\mu_{2}\mu_{1}}(k_{1}+k_{2},-k_{2},-k_{1}).

Appendix B
Kinetic equation for the function
{\mathcal{W}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}(\tau)

Here, we present the kinetic equation for the second color matrix function
{\mathcal{W}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}(\tau)=\bigl({\mathcal{W}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}(\tau)\bigr)
. The kinetic equation follows from the general one (
6.6
) by substituting into it the representation (
6.8
). Then we must contract the left- and right-hand sides of Eq.  (
6.6
) with the Pauli matrix
({\sigma}_{3})^{\alpha^{\prime}\hskip 0.28436pt\alpha}
considering that
{\rm tr}(\sigma_{3})^{2}=2
. As a result we have

\frac{\partial\hskip 1.13791pt{\mathcal{W}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\!(\tau)}{\partial\hskip 0.85355pt\tau}=

(B.1)

\displaystyle-\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\rho}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k}}(t^{\prime})\,\biggl(\int{T}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k}}(t^{\prime})\hskip 0.85355ptdt^{\prime}\biggr)\Bigl\{\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{2}}{\mathcal{W}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436pta\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}\!\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,+

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}{\mathcal{W}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436pta\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}\!\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\Bigr\}\,-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\rho}\,{T}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt({\rho})}_{\;{\bf k}}(t^{\prime})\,\biggl(\int{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k}}(t^{\prime})\hskip 0.85355ptdt^{\prime}\biggr)\Bigl\{\bigl(\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{W}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436pta\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,+

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{W}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436pta\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\Bigr\}\,-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\rho}\hskip 0.56917pt(-1)^{\rho+1}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k}}(t^{\prime})\,\biggl(\int{T}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k}}(t^{\prime})\hskip 0.85355ptdt^{\prime}\biggr)\Bigl\{\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{2}}{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436pta\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}\!\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,+

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436pta\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}\!\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\Bigr\}\,-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\rho}\hskip 0.56917pt(-1)^{\rho+1}\,{T}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt({\rho})}_{\;{\bf k}}(t^{\prime})\,\biggl(\int{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k}}(t^{\prime})\hskip 0.85355ptdt^{\prime}\biggr)\Bigl\{\bigl(\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436pta\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,+

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436pta\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\Bigr\}.

This equation closes the kinetic equation (
6.9
) for the first color matrix function
{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}=({\mathcal{N}}^{\,a\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{\phantom{1}}\!}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}})
in (
6.8
).

Appendix C
Equation for the mean value of color charge
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}

This Appendix provides a general form of the evolution equation for the average value of the second color charge
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}
. The derivation of this equation coincides exactly with the one of a similar equation for the average value of the first color charge
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}
, given in section
9
. It has the following form:

\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}(\tau)\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\rangle}{d\hskip 0.85355pt\tau}=

(C.1)

\displaystyle-\,\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\rho}\,\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k}}(t^{\prime})\biggl(\int{T}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k}}(t^{\prime})\hskip 0.85355ptdt^{\prime}\biggr)\Bigl[

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e^{\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{1}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,-

\displaystyle i\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}\,

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,c^{\prime}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e^{\prime}_{2}}{\mathcal{N}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}_{2}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\Bigr]\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,-

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07182pt\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\rho}\,\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,{T}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k}}(t^{\prime})\biggl(\int{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(\rho)}_{\;{\bf k}}(t^{\prime})\hskip 0.85355ptdt^{\prime}\biggr)\Bigl[

\displaystyle t^{\prime})\hskip 0.85355ptdt^{\prime}\biggr)\Bigl[{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e^{\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{W}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{1}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,-
