Appendix D
Traces for generators in the adjoint representation

In this Appendix, we have given the traces for generators in the adjoint representation, which we use throughout the text of this paper. A comprehensive list of the various traces, relations and identities for the color matrices in the adjoint representation can be found in
[
71
,
72
,
73
,
74
,
75
,
76
]
. The original definition of matrices
T^{\,a}
is

\bigl(T^{\,a}\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}\equiv-\hskip 0.56917pti\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc},

where
f^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}
are the totally antisymmetric structure constants for the
\mathfrak{su}(N_{c})
Lie algebra. These matrices are traceless, i.e.

{\rm tr}\,T^{\,a}=0

and satisfy the following commutation relation

\bigl[\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a},T^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr]=i\hskip 0.56917ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}\,T^{\,c}.

The traces of a product of two and three generators are

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,b}\bigr)=N_{c}\hskip 1.13791pt\delta^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptb},

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,c}\bigr)=\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}.

(D.1)

We will also need the trace of four generators

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,c}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,d}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)=\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptd}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}+\frac{1}{2}\,\bigl(\hskip 0.56917pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.85355ptd}+\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptc}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptd}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)+\frac{1}{4}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl(\hskip 0.56917ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.28436ptf^{\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\!+d^{\hskip 1.13791pta\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\hskip 1.13791ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr),

(D.2)

where
d^{\hskip 1.13791pta\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}
are the totally symmetric structure constants for the
\mathfrak{su}(N_{c})
Lie algebra.
If one uses the relation

f^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.28436ptf^{\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}=\frac{2}{N_{c}}\,\bigl(\hskip 0.56917pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptc}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptd}-\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptd}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)+\bigl(\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.28436ptd^{\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\!-d^{\hskip 1.13791ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.85355ptd^{\hskip 1.13791pta\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr),

(D.3)

then the trace (
D.2
) can also be presented in a slightly different form

{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,c}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,d}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)=\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.85355ptd}+\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptd}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}+\frac{1}{4}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl(\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.28436ptd^{\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\!+d^{\hskip 1.13791pta\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.85355ptd^{\hskip 1.13791ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}-d^{\hskip 1.13791pta\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.85355ptd^{\hskip 1.13791ptb\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr).

(D.4)

The trace (
D.4
) is written in such a form, which shows its symmetry under permutation of the indices
a
and
c
(and, correspondingly,
b
and
d
), i.e.

{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,c}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,d}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)={\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,c}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,d}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)={\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,d}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,c}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr).

(D.5)

The trace of five generators
T^{\,a}
can be represented as a linear combination of the traces of four generators
[
77
]
7
7

7

In the cited paper in formula (45) for the trace of five generators in one of the terms on the right-hand side, two indices are incorrectly placed.

{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)

(D.6)

\begin{split}=-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{i}{2}\,\Bigl\{&f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{4}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 1.42271pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)+f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{5}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 1.42271pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)\\[3.01385pt]
+\,&f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{5}\hskip 0.56917pta_{4}\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 1.42271pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)+f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 1.42271pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)\Bigr\}.\end{split}

This expression is a consequence of the sign reversal property of the permutation of matrices
T^{\,a}
under the trace sign in inverse order

{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)=-{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr),

which, in turn, is a trivial consequence of the identity

{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)=-\hskip 0.85355pt2\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(t^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl[\hskip 0.85355ptt^{\,a_{1}},\bigl[\hskip 0.56917ptt^{\,a_{2}},\bigl[\hskip 0.56917ptt^{\,a_{3}},\bigl[\hskip 0.56917ptt^{\,a_{4}},\bigl[\hskip 0.85355ptt^{\,a_{5}},t^{b}\bigr]\bigr]\bigr]\bigr]\bigr]\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr),

where
t^{\,a}
are the
N^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{c}-1
generators in the defining representation of the
\mathfrak{su}(N_{c})
Lie algebra.

Based on the representation (
D.6
) and considering the symmetry property (
D.5
), it is easy to see that the following useful relation exists for the fifth-order trace under permutations of indices
a_{3}
and
a_{5}
:

{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)+{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)=-\hskip 0.85355ptif^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.28436ptb}\hskip 1.13791pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,b}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr).

(D.7)

We use this relation in section
9
and in Appendix
F
to calculate imaginary parts that appear when determining the equations for the certain colorless combinations constructed from the averaged color charges
\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle
and
\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle
(see the next Appendix).

There are also two additional identities for the special case of
N_{c}=3

[
72
,
76
]
. The first and main of them has the following form:

d^{\,a\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\,c\hskip 0.56917ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}+d^{\,a\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\,b\hskip 0.56917ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}+d^{\,a\hskip 0.56917ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\,b\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}=\frac{1}{3}\,\bigl(\hskip 0.56917pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptb}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.56917ptd}+\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptc}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.56917ptd}+\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptd}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.56917ptc}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr).

(D.8)

This relation can also be presented in a slightly different form

\bigl\{\hskip 0.56917ptD^{\,a},D^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\}^{c\hskip 0.56917ptd}=-\hskip 0.85355ptd^{\,a\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl(D^{\,e}\bigr)^{cd}+\frac{1}{3}\,\bigl(\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.85355ptb}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.85355ptd}+\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.85355ptd}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}+\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.85355ptc}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptd}\bigr),

where
\bigl(D^{\,a}\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}\equiv d^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}
. A useful consequence of it is the identity involving antisymmetric structural constants

\bigl\{\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a},T^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\}^{c\hskip 0.56917ptd}=3\hskip 0.85355ptd^{\,a\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl(D^{\,e}\bigr)^{cd}+\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.85355ptb}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.85355ptd}-\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.85355ptd}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}-\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.85355ptc}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptd}.

(D.9)

Appendix E
Some relations with the averaged color charges for the special case of
N_{c}=3

In the section
7
, we introduced the following combinations involving two averaged color charges:

{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\equiv\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle,\quad{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}\equiv\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle,\quad{\mathfrak{q}}_{12}\equiv\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle,\vskip-8.5359pt

(E.1)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{c}=\Lambda^{c}(\tau)\equiv f^{\hskip 0.85355ptc\,b_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptb_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle,

\displaystyle\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}_{11}=\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}_{11}(\tau)\equiv d^{\,c\,b_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptb^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b^{\prime}_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle,

\displaystyle\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}_{22}=\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}_{22}(\tau)\equiv d^{\,c\,b_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptb^{\prime}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b^{\prime}_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle,

\displaystyle\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}_{12}=\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}_{12}(\tau)\equiv d^{\,c\,b_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptb^{\prime}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b^{\prime}_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle.

These functions are included in the kinetic equations (
7.25
), (
7.26
), (
8.7
) and (
8.8
) in the form of colorless combinations of the type

{\mathfrak{q}}_{1},\;\;{\mathfrak{q}}_{2},\;\;{\mathfrak{q}}_{12},\quad\Lambda^{2}\equiv\Lambda^{e}\Lambda^{e},\quad\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{12}\equiv\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{12}\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{12}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{11}\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{22}.

In this Appendix, for a particular and physically most important case when
N_{c}=3
, we derive formulas that allow us to get rid of at least the last two colorless combinations associated with the symmetric structural constants.

As a first step, we take a look at the relation (
D.3
), valid for an arbitrary
N_{c}
, and contract it with
\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptb}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptd}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle
.
Making use of the definitions (
E.1
), as a result we have

\Lambda^{2}=\frac{2}{N_{c}}\,\bigl({\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}-{\mathfrak{q}}^{2}_{12}\bigr)\,+\,\bigl(\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{11}\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{22}-\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{12}\bigr).

(E.2)

For the
SU(3_{c})
color group in the previous relation, we can eliminate either the colorless combination

\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{11}\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{22}
or the colorless one
\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{12}
. For this purpose, we use the equality (
D.8
). We contract (
D.8
) again with the same combination of the averaged color charges, namely with

\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptb}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptd}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle
, what gives us this time the relation of the form

2\hskip 1.13791pt\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{12}+\,\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{11}\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{22}=\frac{1}{3}\,\bigl(2\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}^{2}_{12}+\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}\bigr).

It enables to exclude in (
E.2
), for the particular case of
N_{c}=3
, either
\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{11}\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{22}
or
\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{12}
. This gives us the following two relations:

\Lambda^{2}={\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}\,-\,3\hskip 1.13791pt\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{12}

(E.3)

and

\Lambda^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}-{\mathfrak{q}}^{2}_{12}\,+\,\frac{3}{2}\,\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{11}\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{22}.

(E.4)

Note that in the first equality, the contribution proportional to
{\mathfrak{q}}^{2}_{12}
exactly reduced. Let us give two more useful relations that follow from (
D.8
) when contracting it with
\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptb}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptd}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle

or

\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptb}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptd}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle
. Here, we get, correspondingly

\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{11}\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{12}=\frac{1}{3}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{12}\quad\mbox{or}\quad\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{22}\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{12}=\frac{1}{3}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{12}.

(E.5)

Finally, we consider another type of the contractions that arise in sections
9
and
10
, namely,

d^{\,b_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\Lambda^{c}\,\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{12}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\quad\mbox{and}\quad d^{\,b_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\Lambda^{c}\,\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{12}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle.

(E.6)

For the sake of specificity, let us examine the first of these. Here, we have

d^{\,b_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\Lambda^{c}\,\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{12}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=\Lambda^{c}\hskip 0.85355ptd^{\,b_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\,d^{\,e\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\equiv

\Lambda^{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl[\frac{1}{3}\,\bigl\{T^{\,a_{1}},T^{\,a_{2}}\bigr\}^{b_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptc}-\frac{1}{3}\,\bigl(\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptc}-\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptc}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptb_{1}}-\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptb_{1}}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptc}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)\biggr]\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle.

In the last step the relation (
D.9
) was used. Obviously, all terms with the Kronecker delta symbol bringing zero contributions by virtue of

\Lambda^{c}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,c}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=\Lambda^{c}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,c}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=0,

and thus the last expression goes into

-\frac{1}{3}\,\Lambda^{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl[f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\,b_{1}\hskip 0.85355pte\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,e\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pt}+f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,b_{1}\hskip 0.85355pte\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\,e\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigr]\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{3}\,f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\,e\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pt}\Lambda^{e}\Lambda^{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=0.

Thus, for the contractions (
E.6
) in the case of
N_{c}=3
, we have the following equalities:

d^{\;b_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\Lambda^{c}\,\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{12}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad d^{\;b_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\Lambda^{c}\,\Omega^{\hskip 0.85355pte}_{12}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=0.

(E.7)

Appendix F
Imaginary contributions to the equation for
\Lambda^{2}

In this Appendix, we give a couple of specific examples of calculating the imaginary contributions to the evolution equation for the colorless combination

\Lambda^{2}
, Eq. (
10.6
), and prove that these contributions vanish for
N_{c}=3
. Consider first the imaginary contributions to the expression (
10.7
). By virtue of the first contraction in (
9.5
), here we have the following chain of relations for the purely imaginary part:

\displaystyle{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,c_{1}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,c_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=

\displaystyle-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{i}{2}\,

\displaystyle{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptb}\,{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,c_{1}}T^{\,a^{\prime\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,c_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=

(F.1)

-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{i}{2}\,{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl[-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{3}{2}\,\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{a_{1}}\!f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{12}\,-\,\frac{1}{4}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{d}\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptb}\,f^{\hskip 0.85355ptb\,a^{\prime\prime}_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,+

\frac{1}{4}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{d}_{12}\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptb}\,d^{\,b\,a^{\prime\prime}_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\biggr],

where we have used the symmetry relation (
D.7
) connecting the fifth-order trace with the fourth-order trace, an expression for a fourth-order trace (
D.2
), and the corresponding definitions for color combinations, Eq. (
E.1
). Obviously, the first term in square brackets on the most right-hand side of (
F.1
) goes to zero. The second term can be represented as follows:

\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{d}\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptb}\,f^{\hskip 0.85355ptb\,a^{\prime\prime}_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=

(F.2)

i\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{e\hskip 0.85355ptd}\Lambda^{e}\Lambda^{d}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\equiv i\hskip 0.85355pt(-1)^{3}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)^{d\hskip 0.85355pte}\Lambda^{e}\Lambda^{d}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle.

Here, in the last step, we rearranged the matrices in parentheses in reverse order, which led to the appearance of the factor
(-1)^{3}
and the permutation of the indices
d
and
e
. Due to the fact that the common multiplier on the right-hand side of (
F.2
) is symmetric in the indices
d
and
e
, as well as in the indices
a_{2}
and
a^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, this expression also turns to zero.

Further, by virtue of the second contraction in (
9.5
), making use of the expression for the trace (
D.2
), here we have the following basis relation for the imaginary part:

i\hskip 0.85355pt{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pte_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,c^{\prime}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,c_{1}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\rangle\,=

(F.3)

i\hskip 0.85355pt{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl[-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{1}{2}\,\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{a_{1}}\!f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\,+\,\frac{1}{4}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e^{\prime}}\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pte_{1}}f^{\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\prime}\,c_{1}\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pt}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,+

\frac{1}{4}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{e^{\prime}}_{12}\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pte_{1}}d^{\,c^{\prime}\,c_{1}\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pt}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\biggr].

The first term in square brackets is zero. The last term in (
F.3
) we will discuss just below when consider the imaginary contributions from contraction (
10.11
). It cancel with the corresponding contribution of this contraction.

Let us now examine the imaginary contributions from the second contraction (
10.11
). We analyze the first contraction in (
10.2
) in complete analogy with the expression (
F.1
). Here, we have

\displaystyle{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e^{\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,c_{1}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,c_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=

\displaystyle-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{i}{2}\,

\displaystyle{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{2}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptb}\,{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,c_{1}}T^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,c_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=

(F.4)

-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{i}{2}\,{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl[2\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{a_{2}}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\,+\,\frac{1}{4}\,N_{c}\hskip 1.13791pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 1.13791pt\Omega^{\,d}_{11}\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{2}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptb}\,d^{\;b\,a^{\prime}_{1}\hskip 0.28436ptd\hskip 0.85355pt}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,\biggr].

The first term in square brackets is obviously zero. The second term for an arbitrary value of
N_{c}
, is nonzero. We consider the special case, when
N_{c}=3
. Then by virtue of the definition of the function
\Omega^{\,d}_{11}
, Eq. (
E.1
), and the relation (
D.8
) we have

d^{\;b\,a^{\prime}_{1}d\hskip 1.13791pt}\hskip 0.56917pt\Omega^{\,d}_{11}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=\frac{1}{3}\,\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,b_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}.

Taking into account this equality and the definition of the function
\Lambda^{a}
, for
N_{c}=3
, instead of (
F.4
) we obtain finally

-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{i}{2}\,{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl(\frac{1}{4}\,\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{a_{2}}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{1}\!\biggr)\equiv 0.

Thus, for the special case of the
SU(3_{c})
color group, this imaginary contribution goes to zero.

It remains for us to consider the imaginary contribution associated with the second contraction in (
10.2
). Using the formula for the fourth-order trace (
D.2
), here we have the following original expression for the imaginary part:

i\hskip 0.85355pt{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,c^{\prime}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a^{\prime}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,e^{\prime}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,c_{1}}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,c_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte^{\prime}_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,=

(F.5)

i\hskip 0.85355pt{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl[\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{3}{2}\,\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{a_{2}}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{12}\,+\,\frac{1}{4}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{d}\hskip 0.28436ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}f^{\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\prime}\,c_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,+

\frac{1}{4}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 1.13791pt\Omega^{\,d}_{12}\hskip 1.13791ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}d^{\,c^{\prime}\,c_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\biggr].

As usual, the first term in square brackets turns to zero. The second term is zero due to the same reasoning that led us to the identity (
F.2
). Finally, we add the last remaining term to the last remaining one in square brackets in (
F.3
), changing, where it is necessary, the color indices of summation:

\frac{i}{4}\hskip 0.85355pt{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355ptN_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 1.13791pt\Omega^{\,d}_{12}\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}+f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}\bigr)d^{\,c^{\prime}\,c_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\,=

\frac{i}{4}\hskip 0.85355pt{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355ptN_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\Lambda^{e}\hskip 1.13791pt\Omega^{\,d}_{12}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,c^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pts\hskip 0.85355pt}f^{\hskip 0.85355pts\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}\bigr)d^{\,c^{\prime}\,c_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pte_{2}}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\equiv\frac{i}{4}\hskip 0.85355pt{N}^{(2)}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355ptN_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\Omega^{\,d}_{12}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\Lambda^{e}\Lambda^{s}f^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,c^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pts\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigr)\hskip 0.85355ptd^{\,c^{\prime}\,c_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc_{1}}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle.

Here, in the last step, we have used the definition for the function
\Lambda^{s}
. Obviously, this relation is zero. Thus, the sum of the last two contributions in (
F.3
) and (
F.5
) turns to zero for an arbitrary value of
N_{c}
. Thus, we have explicitly shown that most of imaginary contributions of the contractions (
9.5
) and (
10.2
) involving the matrix function
\mathcal{N}_{\bf k}
, vanish for
N_{c}=3
. Unfortunately, we are left with two terms (the last term in (
F.1
) and the second term in (
F.3
)) for which we have not been able to prove that they are equal to (or not equal to) zero, even for the special case of
N_{c}=3
.

All of the above reasoning also apply to the imaginary contributions from similar contractions of the type (
9.5
) and (
10.3
) only with another matrix function
\mathcal{W}_{\bf k}
.
